nuclei. This force is incredibly strong but acts over very short distances (about 1
-15
femtometer, or 10   meters).   [2 M]
Characteristics (Any Two)   [2M]
1.   Charge Independence: It acts similarly between different types of nucleons (protons
and neutrons), regardless of their charge.
2.   Non-Predictable: The force can vary depending on the configuration of nucleons,
making it complex and difficult to predict.
Page 14 of 18
--- page 15 ---
3.   Mediated by Gluons: It is transmitted by gluons, which bind quarks together inside
nucleons.
4.   Attractive and Repulsive: Initially attractive at short distances but can become
repulsive at extremely short ranges to prevent nucleons from collapsing into each other.
5.   Responsible for Nuclear Stability: Crucial for holding the atomic nucleus together,
preventing it from disintegrating.

TE mode: This waveguide mode is dependent upon the transverse electric waves, also
sometimes called H waves, characterized by the fact that the electric vector (E) being always
perpendicular to the direction of propagation. [1M]
TM mode: Transverse magnetic waves, also called E waves, are characterized by the fact that the
magnetic vector (H vector) is always perpendicular to the direction of propagation. [1M]
(TEM mode): The Transverse electromagnetic wave cannot be propagated within a waveguide
but is included for completeness.
It is the mode that is commonly used within coaxial and open wire feeders. The TEM wave is
characterized by the fact that both the electric vector (E vector) and the magnetic vector (H vector)
are perpendicular to the direction of propagation. [2M]

Theory behind formation of Newton rings:

If a plano-convex lens is placed such that its curved surface lies on a glass plate, then an air
film of gradually increasing thickness is formed between the two surfaces. When a beam of
monochromatic light is allowed to fall normally om this air film the resultant pattern can be
observed as an alternating dark and bright circular fringes. These circular fringes are formed
because of the interference between the reflected waves from the top and the bottom surfaces
of the air film. These fringes are circular since the air film has a circular symmetry and the
thickness of the film corresponding to each fringe is same throughout the circle. The
interference fringes so formed were first investigated by Newton and hence known as Newton's
rings.

Diameter of Dark and Bright Rings:

Let us consider the thickness of the air film at point Q as t and r, as the radius of the fringe at
that point together with R as the radius of curvature of the lens.

Hence, OC = CQ=R, HO =m

comparison with 2Rt

Physical significance: [4M]
Physical significance of maxwell's |* equation

According to this total electric flux through any closed surface is a times the total charge
0

enclosed by the closed surfaces, representing Gauss's law of electrostatics, As this does not
depend on time, it is a steady state equation. Here for positive p, divergence of electric field is
positive and for negative p divergence is negative. It indicates that p is scalar quantity.

Physical significance of 2" equation

It represents Gauss law of magnetostatic as ¥.B = 0 resulting that isolated magnetic poles

or magnetic monopoles cannot exist as they appear only in pairs and there is no source or sink
for magnetic lines of forces. It is also independent of time i.e. steady state equation.

Physical significance of 3 equation

It shows that with time varying magnetic flux, electric field is produced in accordance with
Faraday is law of electromagnetic induction. This is a time dependent equation.

Physical significance of 4" equation

This is a time dependent equation which represents the modified differential form of
Ampere's circital law according to which magnetic field is produced due to combined effect of
conduction current density and displacement current density.

TE mode: This waveguide mode is dependent upon the transverse electric waves, also
sometimes called H waves, characterized by the fact that the electric vector (E) being always
perpendicular to the direction of propagation. [1M]
TM mode: Transverse magnetic waves, also called E waves, are characterized by the fact that the
magnetic vector (H vector) is always perpendicular to the direction of propagation. [1M]
(TEM mode): The Transverse electromagnetic wave cannot be propagated within a waveguide
but is included for completeness.
It is the mode that is commonly used within coaxial and open wire feeders. The TEM wave is
characterized by the fact that both the electric vector (E vector) and the magnetic vector (H vector)
are perpendicular to the direction of propagation. [2M]

Theory behind formation of Newton rings:

If a plano-convex lens is placed such that its curved surface lies on a glass plate, then an air
film of gradually increasing thickness is formed between the two surfaces. When a beam of
monochromatic light is allowed to fall normally om this air film the resultant pattern can be
observed as an alternating dark and bright circular fringes. These circular fringes are formed
because of the interference between the reflected waves from the top and the bottom surfaces
of the air film. These fringes are circular since the air film has a circular symmetry and the
thickness of the film corresponding to each fringe is same throughout the circle. The
interference fringes so formed were first investigated by Newton and hence known as Newton's
rings.

Diameter of Dark and Bright Rings:

Let us consider the thickness of the air film at point Q as t and r, as the radius of the fringe at
that point together with R as the radius of curvature of the lens.

Hence, OC = CQ=R, HO =m

comparison with 2Rt

Physical significance: [4M]
Physical significance of maxwell's |* equation

According to this total electric flux through any closed surface is a times the total charge
0

enclosed by the closed surfaces, representing Gauss's law of electrostatics, As this does not
depend on time, it is a steady state equation. Here for positive p, divergence of electric field is
positive and for negative p divergence is negative. It indicates that p is scalar quantity.

Physical significance of 2" equation

It represents Gauss law of magnetostatic as ¥.B = 0 resulting that isolated magnetic poles

or magnetic monopoles cannot exist as they appear only in pairs and there is no source or sink
for magnetic lines of forces. It is also independent of time i.e. steady state equation.

Physical significance of 3 equation

It shows that with time varying magnetic flux, electric field is produced in accordance with
Faraday is law of electromagnetic induction. This is a time dependent equation.

Physical significance of 4" equation

This is a time dependent equation which represents the modified differential form of
Ampere's circital law according to which magnetic field is produced due to combined effect of
conduction current density and displacement current density.

c. The nuclear force, also known as the strong force, is one of the four fundamental forces of   4
nature. It binds protons and neutrons (collectively called nucleons) together in atomic
nuclei. This force is incredibly strong but acts over very short distances (about 1
-15
femtometer, or 10   meters).   [2 M]
Characteristics (Any Two)   [2M]
1.   Charge Independence: It acts similarly between different types of nucleons (protons
and neutrons), regardless of their charge.
2.   Non-Predictable: The force can vary depending on the configuration of nucleons,
making it complex and difficult to predict.
Page 14 of 18
--- page 15 ---
3.   Mediated by Gluons: It is transmitted by gluons, which bind quarks together inside
nucleons.
4.   Attractive and Repulsive: Initially attractive at short distances but can become
repulsive at extremely short ranges to prevent nucleons from collapsing into each other.
5.   Responsible for Nuclear Stability: Crucial for holding the atomic nucleus together,
preventing it from disintegrating.

Physical significance: [4M]
Physical significance of maxwell's |* equation

According to this total electric flux through any closed surface is a times the total charge
0

enclosed by the closed surfaces, representing Gauss's law of electrostatics, As this does not
depend on time, it is a steady state equation. Here for positive p, divergence of electric field is
positive and for negative p divergence is negative. It indicates that p is scalar quantity.

Physical significance of 2" equation

It represents Gauss law of magnetostatic as ¥.B = 0 resulting that isolated magnetic poles

or magnetic monopoles cannot exist as they appear only in pairs and there is no source or sink
for magnetic lines of forces. It is also independent of time i.e. steady state equation.

Physical significance of 3 equation

It shows that with time varying magnetic flux, electric field is produced in accordance with
Faraday is law of electromagnetic induction. This is a time dependent equation.

Physical significance of 4" equation

This is a time dependent equation which represents the modified differential form of
Ampere's circital law according to which magnetic field is produced due to combined effect of
conduction current density and displacement current density.

of the neutrons and protons of which they are composed. This decrease in mass (M) is

energy B of a nucleus is the energy required to break the nucleus into free neutrons and
protons.

c. The nuclear force, also known as the strong force, is one of the four fundamental forces of   4
nature. It binds protons and neutrons (collectively called nucleons) together in atomic
nuclei. This force is incredibly strong but acts over very short distances (about 1
-15
femtometer, or 10   meters).   [2 M]
Characteristics (Any Two)   [2M]
1.   Charge Independence: It acts similarly between different types of nucleons (protons
and neutrons), regardless of their charge.
2.   Non-Predictable: The force can vary depending on the configuration of nucleons,
making it complex and difficult to predict.
Page 14 of 18
--- page 15 ---
3.   Mediated by Gluons: It is transmitted by gluons, which bind quarks together inside
nucleons.
4.   Attractive and Repulsive: Initially attractive at short distances but can become
repulsive at extremely short ranges to prevent nucleons from collapsing into each other.
5.   Responsible for Nuclear Stability: Crucial for holding the atomic nucleus together,
preventing it from disintegrating.

b
.   Explain Electric quadrupole moment. Calculate the electric quadrupole moment
for a Calcium nucleus with having atomic number Z = 20. (Larger radius b =
3.5 fm and smaller radius a = 2.8 fm)
Answer: Electric quadrupole moment   [2M]
The electric quadrupole moment is a measure of the deviation of the nucleus from its
spherical symmetry. Under the situation of a deviation, the nucleus can be imagined
CO-   to be an ellipsoid of revolution with its diameter 2b along the axis of symmetry and
2,3;   diameter 2a along the axis perpendicular to this. The quadrupole moment Q of the
BL-   nucleus, when its electric6 charge is uniformly distributed throughout the ellipsoid, is   [6]
3,4   given by
U5
Q is zero for the nuclei having spherical symmetry (a = b) and uniform charge
distribution. The magnitude of electric quadrupole moment depends on the magnitude
of nuclear charge Z, size of the nucleus (magnitudes of b and a) and the extent of
deviation (difference in b and a) from spherical symmetry.

--- page 13 ---
c.
With the help of a suitable graph, explain any two characteristics of variation of
binding energy per nucleon (BE/A) curve with increasing mass number (A).
Answer: The binding energy curve illustrates the stability of atomic nuclei
based on their mass number (A). We can plot mass numbers on X-axis for
ranges from 1 to around 260) and binding energy on Y-axis for ranges from 0
to about 9-10 MeV.   [1M]
CO-
3;
BL-   [4]
5
U5
[1M]
Any two   [2M]
The variation of binding energy per nucleon (BE/A) with increasing mass
number (A) shows distinct trends:
1.   Initial Increase: For light nuclei (small values of A),
BE/A increases as nucleons (protons and neutrons) are added.
********** 13 **********
--- page 14 ---
This means that adding nucleons generally enhances nuclear
stability.
2.   Peak at Iron: As A increases, BE/A continues to rise,
reaching a maximum around iron (A ≈ 56). Iron nuclei are
among the most stable, with the highest binding energy per
nucleon.
3.   Subsequent Decrease: Beyond iron, the BE/A starts to
decline with further increases in A. This indicates that heavier
nuclei are less stable. The decrease is due to the increasing
repulsive electromagnetic force between the larger number of
protons, which is not fully countered by the strong nuclear
force.
4.   Overall Trend: The overall trend illustrates that while
light and medium nuclei benefit from increased stability with
added nucleons, heavy nuclei become less stable, leading to
processes like nuclear fission or radioactive decay.

b
.   Explain Electric quadrupole moment. Calculate the electric quadrupole moment
for a Calcium nucleus with having atomic number Z = 20. (Larger radius b =
3.5 fm and smaller radius a = 2.8 fm)
Answer: Electric quadrupole moment   [2M]
The electric quadrupole moment is a measure of the deviation of the nucleus from its
spherical symmetry. Under the situation of a deviation, the nucleus can be imagined
CO-   to be an ellipsoid of revolution with its diameter 2b along the axis of symmetry and
2,3;   diameter 2a along the axis perpendicular to this. The quadrupole moment Q of the
BL-   nucleus, when its electric6 charge is uniformly distributed throughout the ellipsoid, is   [6]
3,4   given by
U5
Q is zero for the nuclei having spherical symmetry (a = b) and uniform charge
distribution. The magnitude of electric quadrupole moment depends on the magnitude
of nuclear charge Z, size of the nucleus (magnitudes of b and a) and the extent of
deviation (difference in b and a) from spherical symmetry.
********** 10 **********

Electric quadrupole moment for a Calcium nucleus
Given: Z = 20; b = 3.5 fm ; a = 2.8 fm    [1M]
Formula:   [1M]
Q = (2/5) 20 [(3.5)2 - (2.8)2]   [1M]
Q = 35.28 fm   [1M]

c.
With the help of a suitable graph, explain any two characteristics of variation of
binding energy per nucleon (BE/A) curve with increasing mass number (A).
Answer: The binding energy curve illustrates the stability of atomic nuclei
based on their mass number (A). We can plot mass numbers on X-axis for
ranges from 1 to around 260) and binding energy on Y-axis for ranges from 0
to about 9-10 MeV.   [1M]
CO-
3;
BL-   [4]
5
U5
[1M]
Any two   [2M]
The variation of binding energy per nucleon (BE/A) with increasing mass
number (A) shows distinct trends:
1.   Initial Increase: For light nuclei (small values of A),
BE/A increases as nucleons (protons and neutrons) are added.
********** 13 **********
--- page 14 ---
This means that adding nucleons generally enhances nuclear
stability.
2.   Peak at Iron: As A increases, BE/A continues to rise,
reaching a maximum around iron (A ≈ 56). Iron nuclei are
among the most stable, with the highest binding energy per
nucleon.
3.   Subsequent Decrease: Beyond iron, the BE/A starts to
decline with further increases in A. This indicates that heavier
nuclei are less stable. The decrease is due to the increasing
repulsive electromagnetic force between the larger number of
protons, which is not fully countered by the strong nuclear
force.
4.   Overall Trend: The overall trend illustrates that while
light and medium nuclei benefit from increased stability with
added nucleons, heavy nuclei become less stable, leading to
processes like nuclear fission or radioactive decay.
